# Aeon slice tracker

Updated: 2026-11-16 (**S78 local embeddings is designed only, not built**; **S73 is 8/8 built and closed**: the paging convention, `agent.rows`, the ledger and its actor roads, the ledger's own paged read, the management page that reads it, the panel, modal and verbs that write through the daemon's doors, and the close-out walk — 44 browser steps against a real daemon, rc=0 in both modes, findings G-1…G-6). This is the maintained record of slice and task completion.

Built means implementation is recorded and present in this checkout; it does not mean merged or release-ready. Validation is historical evidence, not a fresh test run. “Recorded” covers only the scope stated below. “Not recorded” means completion or validation has not been established, even if a draft, test specification, or WIP file exists.

Rebuilt from complete PLAN.md, ROADMAP.md, TASKS.md and STATUS.md reads, slice task notes, demos, and implementation paths. Task notes and code take precedence over stale summary banners. This checkout contains concurrent, uncommitted work.

Implementation locations below are relative to `aeon/internal/` unless they start with `ui/` or `cmd/`. Task links carry their specifications and validation evidence; implementation locations identify existing code, not a proposed destination.

`agent.migrate` stamps the person, not `system:migrate`; `agent.edit` always mints a ledger row; `agent.changes` added beyond the chat sketch; S72 CF9's one-implementation-two-doors ends **for this page** by operator ruling; the CRUD family tightens to `HumanDoorOnly` with the three-place grep measured; `agent.edit`'s identity-shaped keys left standing and named; `method.go` measured at 2,311 lines and touched with four) plus three learned building it (unknown keys are **ignored** and only a closed set refused, because T3's forward-compat law outranked the design's first sentence; the row type is `store.AgentRow` with its `Bounds` beside the SQL, not `AgentRowSummary` in the door; six fixtures, not four; the seed plane has **three** content writers, not two — `SeedAgentRetire` is the third and mints `seed <slug> retired`; the `migrate` run became **one transaction**, so a mid-run refusal leaves nothing behind; and a table-level `CHECK` must be the **last** item of a SQLite `CREATE TABLE`, measured and now law in the migrations README; **AR4's paths 7 and 8 are not content roads at all** — the CLI's `aeon agent import` and a committed `agent_import` proposal both end in `plugin.ImportChecked`, which writes `plugin_agent`/`plugin_transition` and never the `agent` table, so the tree has SEVEN content roads (the socket's six through one wiring file, and the seed engine) and there is no `cli:cli` stamp and no `desk.CommitActor` read to add; **`agent.changes` refuses TEN keys, not the seven T5's step 3 lists** — step 4's own fixture asks for an invented `sort` key refused naming itself, which a read cannot produce while ignoring `sort`, so the set ships as the seven plus `sort`/`dir`/`q` while unknown keys stay ignored unconditionally; and because `protogen` builds `params` from EVERY fixture including the refusal ones, `contract.ts` lists refused keys (`limit?` on `agent.rows`, `sort?` on `agent.changes`) — the generator's shape, not this plane's permission). **T6's validation is UI-side and the Go suite is unchanged by it:** `npx vitest run src/features/agents` rc=0 (3 files, 31 tests); the neighbour set `test/{i18n,configPagesSmoke,bench-agents,blueprints-screen,ConfigScreen}` rc=0 (8 files, 67 tests) — CU1 moved a door, so its neighbours were run rather than assumed; whole suite `npx vitest run` **rc=1: 1,594 passed, 1 failed, 1 skipped**, the one failure being `test/port.test.ts`'s component census over `src/components/**` missing another session's UNTRACKED `agent-chat/handoffText.ts` (proved by `git status --porcelain`, and this slice added nothing there) — left red, not paid; `npx tsc -b --noEmit --force` rc=1 with **the same 9 errors and no new one** (none in `features/agents`); `npx vite build` rc=0, the new chunk grep-verified in `ui/dist`. The 1,280 px measurement ran through the S72 T4 harness (`bridge.mjs` + `tauri-stub.mjs` + `aeon serve` on a throwaway `XDG_STATE_HOME` with its seed sets applied = 34 agents) under system Chrome; driver and output kept at `aeon/.s73-scratch/walk/` for T8 to promote. **T7's validation is UI-side and the Go suite is unchanged by it:** `npx vitest run src/features/agents` rc=0 (5 files, **55 tests**); `test/{policy,configPagesSmoke,no-socket-in-web-layer}` rc=0 (39) — `policy.test.tsx` reddened first on this task's own guard comment naming a policy authoring method as a hypothetical offender, and was reworded rather than silenced (the PL15 walker reads comments); `npx tsc -b --noEmit --force` rc=1 with **the same nine errors and none under `src/features/agents`**; `npx vite build` rc=0 (5.86 s); `make protogen`/`make check-protogen` rc=0 and `go test -tags sqlite_fts5 ./internal/protocol -run 'Conformance\|Fixture\|Surface'` rc=0 (0.511 s) over deviation 20's two fixtures. Measured and left alone, both: `test/configPagesSmoke.test.tsx` does not list `agents` among its pages (its own comment assigns that to T3's extraction test), and four `conformance/surface/*.json` files sit in `git diff` at `counts.unfiltered 205→207` — T2's and T5's two new methods arriving in the registry count, correct but belonging to those tasks' commits. **T8's close-out rc:** `go build` / `go vet` / `make guard` / `make check-protogen` rc=0; `-count=1` runs rc=0 on `./internal/store/`, `./internal/protocol/ ./internal/agentmgr/ ./internal/agentseed/` and `./cmd/aeon/` (535 s); `./internal/daemon/` rc=1 on five reds **re-proved at pristine HEAD** (`113510d9` worktree: 4/5 reproduced in full; the load-flaky twin fails at HEAD with the identical `UNIQUE constraint failed: policy.id` sentence, the other passes focused there and here). Whole UI `npx vitest run` rc=1 — **164/165 files, 1,618 passed, 1 skipped; the sole red is `test/port.test.ts`** (another session's untracked `agent-chat/handoffText.ts`, named since T6); `npx tsc -b --noEmit --force` = the same nine pre-existing `features/settings` errors, zero under `features/agents`; `npx vite build` rc=0 (twice); the walk `bash aeon/scripts/qa-s73-walk/run.sh` rc=0 both modes over `.s73-scratch/walk-run/` (driver runs 1–4 failed on the driver's own bugs, each fixed where found — the page never caused one). SIZE1 re-censused `wc -l` at close-out: nothing this slice writes crosses 1000 (max `agentsModel.ts` 614); the standing three (`method.go` 2,344, `errors.go` 2,013, `store.go` 1,635) unchanged in kind. ## Tasks by slice

Each task appears once under its slice. Task IDs are specification IDs, not runtime-loop task numbers. A task with recorded validation can still have unverified live, native, external-service, or release criteria. Read the slice limitation above before treating a recorded test result as acceptance.

Acceptance evidence and limits: [S26-roadmap/DEMO.md](S26-roadmap/DEMO.md). A script alone is not proof it was walked. Pre-existing on this base, verified by stashing and re-running (not T1/T2 regressions): `internal/daemon` `TestHandlersVerifyRefusalsAsDataAndErrors`, `TestBrainstormReplayCreatesGoalAndAttachments`, `TestT6WalkBrainstormRestsAndTalks`; `internal/protocol` `TestTheMethodTableHasExactlyTheExpectedMethods` (golden list vs `history.*`). CLI live-daemon fixtures need a short TMPDIR outside the worktree (unix socket cap).

Final whole-tree sweep, 2026-10-30 (HEAD `21e2daec`, `-count=1`, `-tags sqlite_fts5`, short TMPDIR): `gofmt` clean of roadmap files (the three it names — `cmd/aeon/history.go`, `narrow.go`, `proposal_test.go` — are untouched by this slice), `go vet` clean, `go build` ok, **47 packages ok**; the failing packages are all base-owned: the daemon pair above (plus a timing-flaky steer test and an answer-lane post-Close panic in `protocol.handleAnswer`'s resume goroutine, **reproduced identically on base `b5620631` in a throwaway worktree**, the same exposure `queue_reactor.go`'s comment records), `TestTheMethodTableHasExactlyTheExpectedMethods`, and `tools/protogen` `TestRegeneratingProducesNoDiff` (**also reproduced on base** — the wip tree's contract.ts is hand-edited, and regenerating it would delete S24/S26 verbs the app mirrors, so it was left alone per the slice's "no protogen" line).

2026-10-30 (follow-up — the answer-lane post-Close panic the line above names): **fixed, not worked around.** It was a race: a `handleAnswer`/`step.continue` fire-and-forget `continueAnsweredStep` goroutine outlived store (and test) teardown, then wrote the transcript/checkpoint into a store whose `*sql.DB` was already nil — `database/sql` panics, at whichever write the goroutine reaches first, so the location wanders between runs. The fix is a drain, not a lifetime tie: the two spawn sites (`internal/protocol/answer.go`, `internal/protocol/step_stop.go`) now go through `continueAnsweredStepAsync` (`internal/protocol/continuation.go`), which counts each continuation into `Table.continuationWG`; `Table.WaitContinuations` is the single drain. `Daemon.Shutdown` (`internal/daemon/terminal.go`) drains before termCancel/CloseAll, and the two blueprint fixtures (`blueprint_stop_test.go`, `blueprint_stream_test.go`) drain in cleanup before the recovery store closes — a continuation must not outlive the STORE it writes to, only the connection (the design is unchanged). `go test -tags sqlite_fts5 -count=1 ./internal/daemon/...` is 0 panics and the answer-dispatch tests that used to panic (`TestApprovingStartsTheRun`, `TestDenyingReturnsToTheModelAndStartsNothing`, `TestRunStartIsDeniedAtOnceOnAnUnattendedLoop` + subtests) pass. The 3 daemon-suite failures (2 brainstorm, 1 flaky steer) are unchanged — base-owned, verified by stashing this change and re-running.

| [T10 · Services and the preview refusal](S59-execution-environment/tasks/T10.md) | Built | Recorded in task notes (2026-11-03, branch `s59-exec-env-resume`): `services.go` 305 + `health.go` 153 + `servicenv.go` 86, the reconcile network sweep, `internal/daemon/execenv_reconcile.go` 366 (`serviceArgs` through `execenv.Build` so the deny list is not walked around, `daemonProbe`, `orderForTeardown`), and the migration `20261103203000_provision_owner_network.sql` (61) that rebuilds `environment_provision` to admit `owner='network'` — SQLite cannot widen a CHECK in place, and a network with no ledger row is invisible to the only code that cleans up. Provision order is network → each service healthy → app; teardown is the reverse by the driver's rank. No service publishes a host port and none carries a mount (there is no parameter that could ask for either); the app gets `AEON_SERVICE_<NAME>_HOST/_PORT` DERIVED from the declared name and port, and a service's secrets stay in its own container unless the environment lists the reference itself; the values travel in the engine client's process environment while argv carries `--env NAME`. Preview stays refused (T3's `validate.go:457`) with the follow-up brief in the Notes. **23 PASS / 0 FAIL** in the package, `-race` ok; daemon 29.279 s and the CLI environment/spawn guards 10.981 s ok; `make protogen && check-protogen && guard` clean; the store's count pin moved to 40 files / 85 total. Three product bugs the tests caught: the app row was owned by the STEP (the store would refuse it — a latent T9 bug the fakes never validated), `WaitHealthy` checked the pre-ready snapshot and so probed nothing, and the rollback tore down handle-less snapshots and left the loop's network running. **Not run: the service path against a real engine** — this lane is offline with one local image and the tcp probe needs `/bin/bash` in the service's image; no daemon-side TTL/terminal run for services either (T12's demo) |

Four tabs (Environments · Secret references · Provisions · Effective) against the daemon's own doors; capability chips, origin, the legacy badge and the honesty line all rendered from `environment.vocabulary`; every refusal verbatim with the draft kept and `expectedRevision` carried; bind is dry-run → the daemon's statement → `useConfirm`, declining sends nothing, rows update from the response; **no value field exists anywhere** and a planted sentinel never reaches the DOM; filters travel as params. **The pre-flight gap this task needed was built first**: `knownLimits` did not exist on the vocabulary, so `execenv.KnownLimit` + `SharedWorktreeSentence`/`SharedWorktreeLimit` (vocab.go 338), `protocol.KnownLimitView` (772) and the daemon mapper (834) were added, the conformance fixture extended and `contract.ts` regenerated (+5 lines) — the EV15 status is the daemon's, never the panel's. **8 files / 49 tests PASS** (13.92 s), settings-wide **35 files / 353 tests PASS** (43.54 s), `tsc -b --noEmit` clean, and the Go doors still ok (protocol 8.048 s, execenv 27.666 s, `cmd/aeon -run 'Environment|Spawn'` 14.199 s). | [T5 — the shell stays live while a page works](S72-configuration-shell/tasks/T5.md) | Done 2026-11-06 (opened by the operator's own walk: "the page does say loading but the app is locked") | Recorded in task notes — **three separate causes, all measured**. *The lock*: `rpc_call` was a **synchronous** `#[tauri::command]`, which runs on the main thread while `Session::call` parks up to `CALL_TIMEOUT` — so a slow method stopped the window painting at all (the daemon was never the locker: a second connection answered `policy.list` in 0 ms during a 6 s `seed.status`). Now `connect`/`rpc_call`/`disconnect`/`workspace_add·remove·select`/`component_list·read` are `async` with every blocking step — mutex waits included — inside `spawn_blocking`, over a cloneable `SessionHandle`; **a same-day follow-up made the whole command surface async** — `endpoint_path`/`background_presence`/`workspace_list`/`workspace_active` too, memory-only so they answer in the async body with no `spawn_blocking`, and **zero JS change** because `lib/bridge.ts` already awaits every command. The rule ("every command is async") is written in `main.rs`'s header — the file any new command must be registered in anyway — and enforced by `ui/src-tauri/tests/command_threading.rs` (4 tests; walks `src/`, so a command in a file that does not exist yet is covered; pinned to the `generate_handler![]` set so it cannot quietly go blind; every one proven with a planted canary). Following the rule past the command surface **found and fixed two more freezes**: the close handler read `state.session.lock()`, which `connect` holds across a whole connect (so the X froze the window for the length of a connect — now an `AtomicBool` mirror, and `close_policy` unchanged and still pure), and the tray poller held that same mutex across an `inbox.summary` wait, making a wedged daemon a barrier every command queued behind (now: clone a `SessionHandle`, put the guard down, then call — which moved `inbox_count` onto the handle). A `tokio::net` transport rewrite was considered and DECLINED with reasons: one long-lived duplex socket with its own reader thread, no per-call connection to make concurrent, and it would add a tokio dep plus rewrite shutdown semantics three test files pin. *The six seconds*: the seed engine re-read each set's **uncached** source once per record (69.1 ms per `agents.firstParty` read, 8.3 ms per `tools.core`), replaced by `seed/snapshot.go` — one read per operation; the per-record scan helpers are deleted and `snapshot_test.go` asserts each operation reads the source exactly once. Measured `seed.status {set:""}` **6 164 ms → 140 ms** cold (1 520 → 138 warm), `agents.firstParty` 3 960 → 77, `tools.core` 2 050 → 17. *The page*: `PolicySection` asked `{set:""}` (fifteen sets) to render one, now `{set:"policy.corpus"}` (0.02 s). *The menu*: `lib/pageWait.ts` counts what a page is waiting for (its queries and its lazy chunk) against the row's own slug; `PageLayout` sets the kit row's `loading`, which is the kit's loader + `aria-busy`. Verified: `cargo check --all-targets` rc=0; `cargo test --all-targets` **rc=0, 55 passed / 4 ignored** (the first time the shell's own Rust tests were *run* here, not only compiled — which is what validates the shared `next_id` counter against `liveness.rs`/`live_stream.rs`/`remote_transport.rs`); `go vet` clean and the Go seed family + `internal/daemon -run Seed` + `cmd/aeon -run Seed` ok; vitest **161 files / 1567 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed** (8 new in `page-wait.test.tsx`); `tsc -b` = the same 9 pre-existing errors; `vite build` ✓ 6.09 s; real Chrome (bridge + real daemon): policy rendered in **316 ms**, its row `aria-busy` with the kit spinner throughout, navigation away landed mid-flight, 0 page errors. Pre-existing at base and untouched: `internal/toolseed`'s golden-line test (proved with the Go changes stashed). **F-3 closed; F-6 opened** (`EmbeddedDefinition` re-parses per call — most of the 140 ms left); the sweep opened **F-7** (`CALL_TIMEOUT` is 300 s and was chosen when slow meant frozen; it now parks a blocking-pool thread, and the web layer has no timeout of its own) and **F-8** (`setup` is sync by Tauri's signature and does the store read plus an endpoint probe on the main thread before the loop serves anything). Desktop window still unrun (the named gap): the Rust half is proven by construction + compile + the Rust suite + a guard that refuses a sync command, still not by a clicked window |

The window: filtered `COUNT` first, then `ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ? OFFSET ?` as literal text — the bounds hold **no sort vocabulary**, so the one bridge from a wire word to a SQL word cannot answer even the convention's own `name`, and the door refuses `sort`/`dir`/`q` by name instead of accepting them. `sql.NullString`→`*string` all the way to the wire, and the window is `make(...,0,n)` so an empty page marshals `[]`. Six store tests, every row laid through the door that mints it: three actors newest-first (`user:1000`, `cli:cli`, `system:seed-engine` — T3 and T5 meeting), a slug filter whose TOTAL narrows with it, page 9 answering `[]` with `total: 3`, a fresh store and an unknown slug answering the empty-and-not-an-error shape, and the migrate run visible unfiltered with `Slug == nil` while its own derived agent has **no history at all**. Eight door tests through the one seam: the five answer keys and the row's seven, `"slug":null` marshalled and not absent, ten keys refused as −32602 naming themselves, three invented keys IGNORED and succeeding, `{}`/`null`/`{"slug":null}` all answering page 1 at 20, 500 clamped to an echoed 100, and the three roads read back in arrival order |
| [T6 — the page: action bar, table, paging, sorting, search](S73-agent-crud-page/tasks/T6.md) | Built 2026-11-06 | The app's first kit `Table` consumer, and the questions it had to answer were answered in a browser: `AgentsScreen.tsx` 224 (the action bar, the one `agent.rows` call, the honest states, the verb marker T7 replaces), `AgentsTable.tsx` 335 (columns, row menu, three nothings), `agentsModel.ts` 236 (the wire's words and the two quoted refusals), 13 behaviour tests + the family's first `agentsRpc.guard.test.ts` in this folder, 15 tests. Nothing in the browser counts, sorts, filters or slices — the guard scans for `.sort(`/`.filter(`/`.slice(`/`localeCompare(`/`Math.ceil(` and shows its own teeth; the header-click test has the fake answer `sort=slug` with a REVERSED slice, so a local sort would show the opposite list. Controls are built from the ANSWER's `page/pageSize/total`, the sort indicator from its `sort/dir`, and the in-flight signal is the ask disagreeing with the echo (`useRpcQuery`'s `loading` is first-load only). Q2 read the kit's footer and confirmed it in Chrome: total, page-size select (20/50/100 — the three the control can show, which is why `AGENT_PAGE_SIZES` is those three) and Prev/Next, un-clipped. Q3 measured twice and changed the code: the first set was a 2,885 px name column and a 1,500 px shell at a 1,280 px viewport; the shipped one is 1,036 px of table inside a 1,070 px column with no horizontal scroll, `origin` hidden by default and reachable from the Columns control. Q4: the kit debounces, so the page owns no timer (`debounceMs=300`). Deviations 17–19, and the whole UI suite green but for one pre-existing red (`test/port.test.ts` on another session's untracked `agent-chat/handoffText.ts`) |
| [T7 — the side panel, the delete modal, the row's verbs](S73-agent-crud-page/tasks/T7.md) | Built 2026-11-06 | The nine-field hazard test AR5 exists for is in, and it is the file's reason: the fixture's window row and `agent.view`'s answer describe the same slugs with **different words**, so a panel seeded from the cache cannot pass; the test changes one field and asks for `version`, `tools`, `bodyHash`, `policyAgent`, `prompt`, `skills`, `metadata`, both avatar columns, `internal` and `createdAt` back, then closes with a key-for-key census of the posted `fields` against the row's twenty-four. The panel is `create`/`edit`/`view`/`import` on one `SidePanel` (docked, resizable, 720→420), and it holds the **changes**, never a copy of the row: `typing` is sparse, `agentDraftUnder` spreads it over the loaded row, and `agentEditParams` sends twenty-four keys — so no code path can build an edit from less than `agent.view`. The fifteen the door drops are rendered as facts with the sentence naming why, and a first-party row shows `agentmgr`'s own refusal where the Save would have been, with no Save to press. Delete is `useTypeToConfirm` with `confirmValue` the slug: a wrong slug leaves the button dead, the AM4 refusal prints whole beside `ErrorPanel`, the row stays and the table is **not** re-asked (a refusal changed nothing); a success says the slug the daemon named and re-reads. The ledger tab's toggle is one call with `slug` present or absent, and the `migrate` row's NULL slug renders as `every agent` — the unfiltered view being the only place it exists; its pager is built from the answer's own `page`/`pageSize`/`total`, never from arithmetic. Closing mid-write keeps the write flying and re-reads when it lands, and no wording says cancelled. Four kit facts had to be measured rather than assumed (`MultiToggle`'s options are `role="radio"`; a `required` label's `textContent` is `Name*`, so `getByLabelText` needs the star matcher; `DeleteConfirmModal` is an `alertdialog` and emits the kit's own `Panel:clickable-nested-interactive` dev warning from inside itself — **G-1** in S73's own namespace, not this page's to fix; and the catalogs cannot contain an apostrophe because ICU parsing throws **during render**). 12 + 8 new tests, 55 in the folder green; the guard's `PENDING_T7` deleted and replaced by a ten-door census plus five parsers over the Go source and the generated contract |
